*madhu—*of the demon Madhu (or of the month of Caitra); *anta—*the end; *kṛt—*it makes; *sumanasām—*of the sweet-hearted girls (or of the flowers); *āmodaiḥ—*with the aromas; *ghrāṇa—*of the nose; *tarpaṇaḥ—*causes satisfaction; *rādha-ādi-apara-paryāyaḥ—*whose synonyms are Rādhaand so on (or *rādhā-ādya-para-paryāyaḥ—*the following of Rādhā done by whom is the first and the best);; *mādhavaḥ—*Mādhava (or Vaiśākha); *kasya—*to whom; *na priyaḥ—*not dear.

\[A *gopī,* out of fear of her superiors, speaks to her girlfriend using double meanings:\]

**O friend! Who doesn’t like the month of Vaiśākha? It brings the month of Caitra to an end. During Vaiśākha, the aromas of flowers satisfy the nose. This month is also known by other names such as Rādha.**

**O friend! Who doesn’t like Kṛṣṇa? He is the killer of Madhu. He satisfies the noses of the beautiful girls with the fragrances from His body. He is Rādhā’s first and foremost follower.**

### Text 29-31

***…vākye ’ṣṭādaśadhā tv ime (12)***

**These eighteen varieties of** ***dhvani*** **occur in sentences.**

*ime ’ṣṭādaśadhā dhvanayo vākye bhavantīty arthaḥ. aṣṭādaśadhāsya tu avivakṣita-vācyasya dvau bhedau arthāntara-saṅkramitam atyanta-tiraskṛtaṁ ceti. vivakṣita-vācyasya ṣoḍaśa—asaṁlakṣya-krama-vyaṅgya ekaḥ, saṁlakṣya-krama-vyaṅgyaṁ ca pañcadaśa. tatra śabda-śakty-udbhavo dvau, artha-śakty-udbhavā dvādaśa, ubhaya-śakty-udbhava ekaḥ.*

The meaning is that these *dhvanis,* which are of eighteen varieties, occur in a statement. The eighteen varieties of *dhvanis* are as follows: two kinds of *avivakṣita-vācya* \[which is also called *lakṣaṇa-mūla-dhvani*\], namely *arthāntara-saṅkramita* and *atyanta-tiraskṛta* (3.3-4), and sixteen kinds of *vivakṣita-vācya* \[which is also called *abhidhā-mūla-dhvani*\] (3.8-10).

These sixteen kinds *vivakṣita-vācya dhvani* are as follows: *asaṁlakṣya-krama-vyaṅgya* (where the *krama* (“the sequence of appearance and disappearance”) of the suggestion isn’t perceivable) and fifteen varieties of *saṁlakṣya-krama-vyaṅgya* (where the the *krama* of the suggestion is perceivable).

Among those fifteen, *śabda-śakti-bhū dhvani* is twofold (i.e it suggests an *alaṅkāra* or a *vastu* (3.12)), *artha-śakti-bhū dhvani* is twelvefold,and there is one kind of suggestion arising from *śabdārthobhaya-śakti.*

***vākya eva dvi-śakty-utthaḥ (13)***

**A suggestion arising from** ***ubhaya-śakti*** **can occur only in a sentence.**

*śabdārthobhaya-śakty-udbhavo dhvanir vākya eva. “idha vṛndāaṇa-majjhe” (2.36) ity-ādi tad-udāharaṇam.*

The suggestion that arises from both the power of a word and the power of a meaning occurs only in a sentence. An example of this is verse 2.36.

(The verse and the translation are presented again:)

***iha vṛndāvana-madhye niḥśaṅka-nisupta-mayūra-mṛga-nikaraḥ***  
***ali-mātra-bhukta-kusumo ramaṇīyo yāmuna-kuñjaḥ***

*iha—*in this; *vṛndāvana-madhye—*within Vṛndāvana; *niḥśaṅka—*fearlessly; *nisupta—*who sleep; *mayūra—*of peacocks; *mṛga—*of deer; *nikaraḥ—*there is a multitude; *ali—*bees; *mātra—*only; *bhukta—*are enjoyed; *kusumaḥ—*\[in which *kuñja*\] the flowers; *ramaṇīyaḥ—*charming; *yāmuna—*related to the Yamunā; *kuñjaḥ—*

**In this forest of Vṛndāvana the shore of the Yamunā, where the peacocks and deer sleep without any fear and the flowers are enjoyed only by the bees, is especially charming.**

### Text 32

*…pade saptadaśāpare (13)*

**The other seventeen kinds can also occur in a word.**

*ubhaya-śakty-utthaṁ vināpare saptadaśa pade ’pīty arthaḥ. tathā ca—*

The other seventeen *dhvanis,* apart from *ubhaya-śakti-bhū,* can also occur in a word. For example:

***pada-dyotyena su-kaver***  
***dhvaninā bhāti bhāratī***  
***ekenaiva prasūnena***  
***navevodyāna-ketakī***

*pada-dyotyena—*suggested by a word; *su-kaveḥ—*of a good poet; *dhvaninā—*by the suggestion; *bhāti—*appears beautiful (“shine”); *bhāratī—*words; *ekena—*one; *eva—*only; *prasūnena—*because of a flower; *navā—*young; *iva—*just like; *udyāna—*in a garden; *ketakī—*a tree of *ketakī* flowers.

**The words of a good poet appear beautiful on account of a hint, which is suggested by a word, just like a young** ***ketakī*** **tree in a garden appears beautiful even on account of one flower.**

*iti.*

Here ends the discussion of the theory.

### Text 33

*tatra diṅ-mātram udāhriyate—*

In that regard, merely the direction is illustrated.

***pārṣadāḥ pārṣadā yasya***  
***lakṣmīr lakṣmīḥ kṛpā kṛpā***  
***avatāro ’vatāraś ca***  
***sa devaḥ kair na sevyate***

*pārṣadāḥ—*associates; *pārṣadāḥ—*always by His side; *yasya—*whose; *lakṣmīḥ—*opulence; *lakṣmīḥ—*always present; *kṛpā—*mercy; *kṛpā—*unconditional; *avatāraḥ—*descent; *avatāraḥ—*devoid of birth and death; *ca—*and; *saḥ—*that; *devaḥ—*Lord; *kaiḥ—*by who; *na sevyate—*is not served.

**The Lord’s associates are always by His side, His opulences are always present, His mercy is unconditional, and His descent is devoid of birth and death. Who won’t serve that Lord?**

*atra dvitīya-pārṣadādi-śabdā nirantara-pārśva-sthatva-nirapāyitva-nirupādhitva-janma-maraṇa-bhāva-rahitatveṣv arthāntareṣu saṅkramitāḥ.*

In this verse, the literal meaning of the second word *pārṣadāḥ* has transformed into the figurative meaning “always situated near.” The second word Lakṣmī has transformed into the figurative meaning of “never leaving.” The second word *kṛpā* has transformed into the figurative meaning of “causelessness.” The second word *avatāra* has the sense of “devoid of birth, death, and material existence.”

### Text 34

\[This verse is an example of *atyanta-tiraskṛta lakṣaṇā-mūla dhvani*:\]

***tavānukampā tu tavaiva śobhate***  
***mamāpi daurjanyam aho mamāpi hi***  
***ratir na dīrghā mama dīrgham eva te***  
***prema priyāhaṁ tava kṛṣṇa kiṁ bruve***

*tava—*Your; *anukampā—*mercy; *tu—*but; *tava—*to You; *eva—*only; *śobhate—*appears; *mama—*my; *api—*even; *daurjanyam—*badness (“the quality of being a bad person”); *aho—*how amazing; *mama—*to me; *api—*also; *hi—*only; *ratiḥ—*love; *na—*is not; *dīrghā—*deep; *mama—*my; *dīrgham—*deep; *eva—*only; *te—*Your; *prema—*love; *priyā—*dear beloved; *aham—*I am; *tava—*Your; *kṛṣṇa—*O Kṛṣṇa; *kim—*what; *bruve—*\[can\] I say.

\[An angry *gopī* speaks ironically to Kṛṣṇa:\]

**Your mercy is unique to You. And how amazing! My badness is unique to me. My love is shallow whereas Your love is very deep. I am Your beloved.**

**O Kṛṣṇa! What can I say?**

*atrānukampānanukampā, daurjanyam adaurjanyam, na dīrghā dīrghā, dīrgham adīrgham, priyāpriyeti atyanta-tiraskṛtam ity ubhaya-pada-gamyam. “phalam api phalam ākandānām” (3.5) ity-ādau, “saubhāgyam etad adhikam” (3.7) ity-ādau cobhayor vākya eva viśrāntir iti bhedaḥ.*

Here, the words *anukampā, daurjanyam, na dīrghā, dīrgham,* and *priyā* mean just the opposite of their literal meanings. In this way, the literal meanings’ being completely obliterated should be understood.In the fifth and seventh verses of this chapter, beginning *phalam api phalam* and *saubhāgyam etad adhikam* respectively, the *dhvani* rested only in the *vākya* (“sentences”). That is the difference.

### Text 35

\[This verse is an instance of *abhidā-mūla dhvani* that is *ubhaya-śakti-bhū* and that suggests a *vastu:*\]

***Prākṛta:***  
***taṁ va-aṇaṁ so paphaṁso taṁ rūaṁ taṁ sarīra-sorabbhaṁ***  
***te ahara-mahurimāṇo dāṇiṁ hālāhalaṁ jāaṁ***  
***Saṁskṛta:***  
***tad vacanaṁ sa sparśas tad rūpaṁ tac charīra-saurabhyam***  
***te ’dhara-madhurimedānīṁ hālāhalaṁ jātam***

*tat—*those; *vacanam—*words; *saḥ—*that; *sparśaḥ—*touch; *tat—*that; *rūpam—*form; *tat—*that; *śarīra-saurabhyam—*fragrance of the body; *te—*Your; *adhara—*of the lips; *madhurimā—*sweetness; *idānīm—*now; *hālāhalam—*poison; *jātam—*have become.

**Those words, that touch, that form, that bodily fragrance, and that sweetness of His lips have now become poison.**

*atra tad-ādi-padāny anubhava-gocarān arthān prakāśya pūrvam amṛta-vad āsīt sarvam iti vastu prakāśayantīti pada-gato dhvaniḥ.*

Here the words *tad* and so on (i.e *tad, saḥ, tad, tat,* and *te*), referring to things that are within the realm of experience, convey the following idea (*vastu*): “All these things were previously like nectar.” In this way, the suggestion is attained by the words (i.e because of the term *idānīm,* the suggestion is included in the words, and there is no dependence on another *vākya*).

### Text 36

*śabda-śakty-udbhavo yathā—*

This verse is an instance *śabda-śakti-bhū dhvani:*

***mugdhe padmini kulaje mā kārṣīr atra viśvāsam***  
***anavasthito ’ti-madataḥ so ’yaṁ sākṣād aneka-paḥ kṛṣṇaḥ***

*mugdhe—*O innocent girl; *padmini—*O lotuslike girl; *kula-je—*O you who are born in a respectable family; *mā akārṣīḥ—*don’t do (i.e don’t put); *atra—*in this \[Kṛṣṇa\]; *viśvāsam—*faith; *anavasthitaḥ—*very unsettled (i.e loose in His conduct); *ati-madataḥ—*because of a lot of *mada* (or because of great pride); *saḥ ayam—*this well-known; *sākṣāt—*directly; *aneka-paḥ—*elephant (see explanation below); *kṛṣṇaḥ—*Kṛṣṇa.

**O innocent, lotuslike girl! You are born in a respectable family. Don’t trust that Kṛṣṇa. He is just an elephant that has become maddened by** ***mada*****.**

*atrāneka-pa-śabda ekārtho ’pi kavi-nibaddha-vaktṛ-prauḍhokti-vaśād anekārthatāṁ gataḥ san mugdhe ity-ādi-sambodhana-trayārthena sādhanena sādhyaṁ sva-gatam anavasthitatvādikam. yathā—tvaṁ mugdhā, ayam anavasthitaḥ, tvaṁ kula-jā, ayam ati-madaḥ, tvaṁ padminī, ayam aneka-po hastīty artha-trayaṁ bodhayati. anekaṁ pātīti, anekena pibatīti ca, aneka-vadhū-patir matta-hastī ca. padminīti nāyikā-viśeṣaḥ, kamalinīti ca. tvaṁ kula-jā, ayam anekaṁ pibatīti mattaś ca, tenātra viśvāsaṁ mā kārṣīr yato ’ti-madato ’navasthitaḥ. mado dānaṁ garvaś ca, tena tvām avaśyaṁ mardayiṣyatīti vastv-anantaraṁ vastv-antaraṁ ca. hasti-sādharmyād upamālaṅkāraḥ, anavasthita iti hetv-alaṅkāraḥ, anekasya bharteti svabhāvokty-alaṅkāraḥ—ity alaṅkāra-vyañjakaḥ śabda-śakty-udbhavaḥ pada-gataḥ.*

In this verse, the word *aneka-pa,* although it has only one meaning (i.e by conventional meaning it only means “an elephant”), becomes a word with multiple meanings due to the fact that this is a *kavi-nibaddha-vaktṛ-prauḍhokti* (i.e the uncommon statement of a speaker created by the poet). Kṛṣṇa’s qualities such as being unsettled and so on are directly caused by the qualities mentioned in the three vocatives beginning with *mugdhe,* i.e He is unsettled because you are innocent; He’s totally mad because you’re from a respectable family, and He’s an elephant because you’re a lotus.

In this verse, the word *aneka-pa* suggests these three meanings: i) *anekaṁ pāti* (“He maintainss many”), which refers to the master of many young girls; ii) *anekena pibati* (“It drinks through more than one, i.e It drinks through his mouth and trunk; or he drinks along with many, i.e It drinks along with women and children”),which refers to a mad elephant; and iii) *anekaṁ pibati* (“He drinks many, i.e He drinks the lips of many girls”), which refers to Kṛṣṇa who is mad with lust.

The designation *padminī* refers to one of the four kinds of heroines. It also means “a lotus.” Moreover, \[another contrast is:\] You are an aristocratic girl, whereas He drinks the lips of many girls and is thus mad with lust. Therefore do not put your trust in Him, because He is unsettled due to excessive *mada.*. The word *mada* signifies rut fluid or pride. Therefore the idea is, “\[Because you are a lotus and He is an elephant\] He will certainly crush you.” In this way there is a *vastu* coming from another *vastu.*

Because of similarity between Kṛṣṇa and elephants, there is the *alaṅkāra* called *upamā* (simile)*.* Since His being badly behaved is a cause, there is a *hetv-alaṅkāra* (cause)*.* Moreover, by the meaning of *aneka-pa* in the sense of Kṛṣṇa’s being a lover of many girls, there is the *alaṅkāra* called *svabhāvokti* (a statement describing a special characteristic). In this way, the suggestions of these *alaṅkāras* arise by *śabda-śakti* (“the power of words”)and they are *pada-gata* (i.e the suggestions are contained within the words themselves and are not dependent upon another *vākya*).

### Text 37

*pada-gatārtha-śakty-udbhavaḥ svataḥ-sambhavī yathā—*

This verse is an example of *abhidhā-mūla artha-śakti-bhū dhvani* based on the words themselves, where the suggestion is *svataḥ-sambhavī* (see 3.15):

***Prākṛta:***  
***ṇihu-aṇa-kadhāhiṁ dhaṇṇā ṇia-parivāraṁ suhāventi***  
***apapāṇaṁ pi ṇa hu tadā sumara-idāṇiṁ bhaṇādu kiṁ bhodī***  
***Saṁskṛta:***  
***nidhuvana-kathābhir dhanyā nija-parivāraṁ sukhāpayanti***  
***ātmānam api na hi tadā smaratīdānīṁ bhaṇatu kiṁ bhavatī***

*nidhuvana—*about sexual union; *kathābhiḥ—*by talks; *dhanyāḥ—*are fortunate; *nija-parivāram—*own associates; *sukhāpayanti—*they make happy; *ātmānam—*the body; *api—*even; *na hi—*doesn’t; *tadā—*at that time; *smarati—*she remembers; *īdānīm—*now; *bhaṇatu—*can say; *kim—*what; *bhavatī—*you.

\[Several *gopīs* address their group leader:\]

**By talking about their sexual union, those fortunate girls make their friends happy. But in your case, since during union you’re not even aware of your own body, what can you say now?**

*atra na tā dhanyās tvam eva dhanyā, tāsāṁ sakhībhyo vayam ati-sukhinyaḥ, tvad-ānandāveśād eva vayam akathanenāpy ānanda-bhājaḥ. tās tu tat kathayaiveti dhanyā-pada-gataḥ svataḥ-sambhavī. apare tūhyā, grantha-gaurava-bhayān nodāhriyante. vākya-gatāḥ pūrvam eva kiyanto darśitāḥ.*

Here what is suggested is as follows: “They aren’t fortunate, only you are fortunate.We, your friends, are happier than the friends of those girls. Simply by being absorbed in your bliss, we partake of the bliss even without talks about union. But those friends of other girls become blissful only through such talks.” In this way, there is *svataḥ-sambhavī dhvani* arising from the word *dhanyāḥ.*

The other examples of *pada-dhvani* should be inferred in the same way, but they won’t be illustrated out of fear of increasing the size of this book. The examples of *vākya-dhvani,* however, were fully illustrated previously (3.5-28).

### Text 38-40

***pañca-triṁśat tato bhedāḥ***

Therefore, there are thirty-five varieties.

**tato ’ṣṭādaśabhiḥ saptadaśabhiś cety arthaḥ.**

This statement means: Therefore, because of eighteen varieties and seventeen varieties, there is a total of thirty-five varieties.

***…prabandhe ’py artha-śakti-bhūḥ***

The *artha-śakti-bhū dhvani* can also be found in *prabandhas.*

***artha-śakty-udbhavo dvādaśa-vidho dhvaniḥ prabandhe ’pi.***

This statement means: The twelve varieties of *artha-śakti-bhū dhvani* are also found in *prabandhas.*

***sapta-catvāriṁśad ataḥ (14)***

**Therefore there are forty-seven varieties \[of** ***dhvanis*** **in** ***prabandhas*****\].**

***ato hetoḥ sapta-catvāriṁśad bhavanti.***

This statement means: because of this, there are forty-seven varieties.

### Text 41

***prabandhe diṅ-mātram udāhriyate—***

*Now merely the direction of how dhvanis are used in prabandhas is illustrated.*

***Prākṛta:***  
***sahanti gandhaṁ vi ṇa vai jaṇāṇaṁ ṇava-ppasūā sa-alā hi gāo***  
***na teṇa doho ṇa pa-a-ppasaṅgo ajje bahūo tuha viṇṇaventi***  
***Saṁskṛta:***  
***sahante gandham api na vai janānāṁ nava-prasūtāḥ sakalā hi gāvaḥ***  
***na tena doho na payaḥ-prasaṅga ārye vadhvas tvāṁ vijñāpayanti***

*sahante—*tolerate; *gandham—*the scent; *api—*even; *na—*cannot; *vai—*indeed; *janānām—*of people; *nava—*recently; *prasūtāḥ—*that have given birth; *sakalāḥ—*all; *hi—*indeed; *gāvaḥ—*cows; *na—*there is no; *tena—*therefore; *dohaḥ—*milking; *na—*there is no; *payaḥ—*for milk; *prasaṅgaḥ—*occasion; *ārye—*O respectable lady; *vadhvaḥ—*the young brides; *tvām—*you; *vijñāpayanti—*inform.

**O respectable lady! The young brides wish to inform you that all the cows that have recently given birth cannot even tolerate the scent of anyone. Therefore, since they cannot be milked, there is no milk available.”**

*atra vadhūbhiḥ preṣita-śvaśrū-dhātryā naptrī tāsāṁ śvaśrūṁ prati kathayati.*

Here, the context is: The grand-daughter of the mother-in-law’s old nurse was sent by the young brides. She informs those girls’ mother-in-law.

### Text 42-43

*tac chrutvā sāpi jaratī tām āha—*

***Prākṛta:***  
***karemi kiṁ ṇattiṇi dhattiāe vaessarīṁ lambhia viṇṇavehi***  
***tuhāṇa savvaṁ maha gohaṇādi dhaṇaṁ jaṇāo vi suhaṁ ca dukkhaṁ***  
***su-duppaāhā mada savva gāo puttā vidūre kim ahaṁ karemi***  
***viloaṇādo tuha ṇandaṇassa su-duppaāhāvi sa-appaohā***

***Saṁskṛta:***  
***karomi kiṁ naptri dhātryā***  
***vrajeśvarīṁ labdhvā vijñāpaya***  
***yuṣmākaṁ sarvaṁ mama go-dhanādi-***  
***dhanaṁ janā api sukhaṁ ca duḥkham***  
***su-duṣpradohā mama sarva-gāvaḥ***  
***putrā vidūre kim ahaṁ karomi***  
***vilokanāt tava nanda-nandanasya***  
***su-duṣpradohā api svayaṁ-pradohāḥ***

*karomi kim—*what can I do; *naptri—*O grand-daughter; *dhātryāḥ—*of a nurse; *vraja-īśvarīm—*the queen of Vraja; *labdhvā—*having approached; *vijñāpaya—*you should inform; *yuṣmākam—*your; *sarvam—*all; *mama—*my; *go-dhana-ādi—*such as the wealth of cows and so on; *dhanam—*the wealth; *janāḥ—*people; *api—*also; *sukham—*happy; *ca—*and; *duḥkham—*unhappy.

*su-duṣpradohāḥ—*are very difficult to milk; *mama—*of mine; *sarva-gāvaḥ—*all the cows; *putrāḥ—*sons; *vidūre—*are far away; *kim aham karomi—*what can I do; *vilokanāt—*because of seeing; *tava—*your; *nanda-nandanasya—*the son of Nanda; *su-duṣpradohāḥ—*\[cows that are\] easy to milk; *api—*even; *svayam-pradohāḥ—*\[cows that\] give milk by themselves.

Hearing these words, that old mother-in-law says to the grand-daughter of the old nurse:

**O grand-daughter of my nurse! What can I do? Go to the queen of Vraja and inform her as follows: “All of your wealth such as cows and so on is well, and your people are happy, whereas my people are unhappy and my wealth of cows is uneasy. All my cows are difficult to milk. My sons are far away. But just by seeing Nanda’s son, even the cows that are most difficult to milk give milk by themselves.”**

### Text 44

*iti jaraty-uttarānte goṣṭheśvarīṁ gatvā tayā yad uktaṁ tat kathayati—*

***Prākṛta:***  
***evvaṁ kkhu tāe bhaṇidā gadāhaṁ vaessarīṁ uttavadī samatthaṁ***  
***utteṇa tāe kumareṇa uttaṁ gosaṁ ppaoho mama ṇa kkhu hoi***

***Saṁskṛta:***  
***evaṁ khalu tayā bhaṇitā gatāhaṁ vrajeśvarīm uktavatī samastam***  
***uktena tayā kumāreṇoktaṁ gosaṁ pradoho mama na khalu bhavati***

*evam—*in this way; *khalu—*indeed; *tayā—*by her (the old lady); *bhaṇitā—*being addressed; *gatā aham—*I went; *vraja-īśvarīm—*to the queen of Vraja; *uktavatī—*\[and\] told; *samastam—*everything; *uktena—*who was spoken to; *tayā—*by her (Mother Yaśodā); *kumāreṇa—*by the son; *uktam—*what was said; *gosam—*in the morning; *pradohaḥ—*milking; *mama—*done by me; *na—*not; *khalu—*indeed; *bhavati—*is.

After hearing the old lady’s words, the grand-daughter of the old nurse goes to meet the queen of Vraja. Then, the young girl comes back and tells \[the young brides\] what was said by Mother Yaśodā \[to Kṛṣṇa\]:

**Being thus addressed by the old lady, I went to Mother Yaśodā and told her everything she said. This is what Kṛṣṇa replied after Yaśodā spoke to Him: “I don’t milk cows in the morning.”**

*iti catuḥ-saṁvāda-prabandhe vadhūbhiḥ kṛṣṇa-sandarśanārtham upāyam anavekṣya prākaraṇika-duṣpradoha-go-dohābhāva-prasaṅgaḥ śvaśrūṁ prati vijñāpitaḥ. atrāpahnuti-nāmālaṅkāraḥ. tathā ca go-dohopāyaṁ cintayitvā he dhātrikā-naptri, goṣṭheśvarīṁ gatvā vijñāpayety uttaraṁ dattam ity uttarālaṅkāraḥ.*

*tataś ca “su-duṣpradohā mama sarva-gāvaḥ” iti svabhāvokty-alaṅkāraḥ. vilokanād ity-ādinātiśayoktiḥ. tathā ca sukha-dohe pariśramas tasya na bhaviṣyatīti vastu. gosaṁ pradohaḥ prabhāta-pradoho matto na bhavatīti niścayālaṅkāreṇa sāyaṁ-dohas tu mayā kartavya iti vadhūnām āśayaṁ jñātvā chalena saṅketaḥ kṛta iti vastu—iti catuḥ-ślokyā samuditaḥ prabandho hi mukhyo vyañjakaḥ.*

*tathā hi “janānāṁ gandham api na sahante” ity anyair adohyatvaṁ vastu. atra nava-prasūtā iti hetor hetv-alaṅkāraḥ. tena “kṛṣṇaṁ vinā nāsāṁ dohaḥ” iti śvaśrvā kṛṣṇa ānāyitavya iti vastv-antaram, “na tena doha” ity anena payo-’bhāvād vayam akriyāḥ smaḥ. atra mā kopaṁ kārṣīr iti ca vastv-antaram. “tuhāṇa savvam” ity-ādinā vinaya-mahimnā kṛṣṇo ’vaśyaṁ vrajeśvaryā preṣayitavya iti vastu.*

*evaṁ nāṭakādiṣu ceti tad-bheda-kathanenānantya-prasaṅga iti nātra likhitaḥ. evaṁ prabandhe ’py anya ekādaśāpi boddhavyā.*

In this *prabandha* that contains four conversations,the young brides, not seeing any way to meet Kṛṣṇa, inform the mother-in-law that the cows, which are difficult to milk, aren’t giving milk. In this regard, there is the ornament of meaning (*alaṅkāra*) called *apahnuti* (concealment of the truth).Furthermore the *alaṅkāra* called *uttara* (giving an answer) occurs because the mother-in-law, thinking of a way to milk the cows, gives the answer “O grand-daughter of my old nurse, go to the queen of Vraja and inform her of the situation.”

Additionally, because of the words “All my cows are very difficult to milk,” there is the *alaṅkāra* called *svabhāvokti* (description ofa special characteristic). Moreover, the words “just by seeing Nanda’s son, even the cows that are most difficult to milk give milk by themselves (3.43)” is an instance of the *alaṅkāra* called *atiśayokti* (“a statement of superexcellence”). In this way we get the idea (*vastu*) that Kṛṣṇa won’t have to exert Himself while performing such easy milking.

There is also the *alaṅkāra* called *niścaya* (“firm decision”) due to Kṛṣṇa’s words, “I don’t milk cows in the morning.” Because of this statement, it’s understood that Kṛṣṇa means “I milk in the evening.” In this way there is a *vastu:* Knowing the young brides’ intention, Kṛṣṇa, on a pretext, fixed the time for their meeting. These are the main suggestions in this *prabandha* composed of four verses.

Other suggestions are as follows. By the words “All the cows that have recently given birth cannot even tolerate the scent of anyone (3.41),” there is the idea (*vastu*) that these cows cannot be milked by anybody other than Kṛṣṇa. The reason why these cows cannot be milked by anybody else is that they have just given birth. This is an example of the *alaṅkāra* called *hetu* (“cause”).

There is also another *vastu:* Since the cows cannot be milked without Kṛṣṇa, He should be brought by the mother-in-law.Because of the words “milking cannot be done (3.41),” an additional *vastu* arises, i.e the young brides say “Due to the lack of milk, we are left without work. Don’t be angry at us for this.” Lastly, with the words “All of your wealth such as cows and so on is well and your people are happy (3.42),” there is the idea that Kṛṣṇa will definitely be sent by the queen of Vraja due to the greatness of her behavior.

There would be an infinite number of varieties if we also discussed the varieties of suggestions in dramas and so on. Thus we have not written of them here.. The other eleven types of *artha-śakti bhū dhvani* occuring in a *prabandha* should also be understood in the same way.

### Text 45-47

***…padāṁśādyā rasāñjakāḥ (14)***

*pada-aṁśa—*parts of a word; *ādyāḥ—*those whose beginning is; *rasa—rasa; añjakāḥ—*suggest.

**Parts of a word and so on are suggestive of** ***rasa.***

*padāṁśa-varṇa-racanā iti tritayam api rasasya vyañjakaṁ bhavati. raso ’trāsaṁlakṣya-kramaḥ.*

This *kārikā* means: Parts of a word, certain phonemes,and different styles of compositionsgive rise to *rasa.* Here the word *rasa* refers to anything whose order of appearance and disappearance is not perceivable (3.8-10).

***tena tasya trayo bhedāḥ prabandhe ’pi sa kathyate (15)***

*tena—*therefore; *tasya—*of that \[*rasa*\]; *trayaḥ—*three; *bhedāḥ—*varieties; *prabandhe—*in a short story; *api—*also; *saḥ—*that; *kathyate—*is said.

**Therefore there are three varieties.** ***Asaṁlakṣya-krama*** **is also found in** ***prabandhas.***

*so ’saṁlakṣya-kramaḥ.*

The word *saḥ* in the *kārikā* refers to *asaṁlakṣya-krama,* i.e that whose order of appearance and disappearance is not perceivable.

***bhedās tenaika-pañcāśat (16)***

**Therefore there are fifty-one varieties.**

*tena pūrva-likhita-sapta-catvāriṁśatā caturbhir etaiś caika-pañcāśad bhavanti.*

There are fifty varieties because these four kinds (i.e *padāṁśa, varṇa, racanā,* and *asaṁlakṣya-krama* in a *prabandha*) are added to to the forty-seven kinds mentioned previously (3.38-40).

### Text 48-51

*padāṁśāḥ padaika-deśāḥ. te ca—*

The term *padāṁśa* refers to “one place of a word.” The *padāṁśas* are as follows:

***prakṛtiḥ pratyayaḥ kālo***  
***vartamānādir eva yaḥ***  
***sambandho vacanaṁ cāpi***  
***puruṣa-vyatyayo ’pi ca***

***taddhitaṁ copasargaś ca***  
***nipātaḥ sarvanāma ca***  
***karma-bhūtādhikaraṇam***  
***avyayī-bhāva eva ca***

***tathā pūrva-nipātaś ca***  
***padāṁśāḥ parikīrtitāḥ***  
***racanā ca tridhā dīrgha-***  
***madhya-rikta-samāsataḥ***

*prakṛtiḥ—*the base; *pratyayaḥ—*the suffix; *kālaḥ—*time; *vartamāna-ādiḥ—*the present tense and so on; *eva—*only; *yaḥ—*which; *sambandhaḥ—sambandha*; *vacanam—*the number; *ca—*and; *api—*also; *puruṣa-vyatyayaḥ—*the irregular switching of the persons; *api—*also; *ca—*and; *taddhitam—*the *taddhita pratyaya; ca—*and; *upasargaḥ—*the prefix; *ca—*and; *nipātaḥ—*a *nipāta* (HNV *Bṛhat* 364); *sarvanāma—kṛṣṇanāmas; ca—*and; *karma-bhūta—*that has become a *karma; adhikaraṇam—*an *adhikaraṇa*; *avyayī-bhāvaḥ—avyayī-bhāva* compounds (HNV 981-983); *eva—*only; *ca—*and; *tathā—*in the same way; *pūrva-nipātaḥ—a samāsa* in which the order of the words is reversed; *ca—*and; *pada-aṁśāḥ—*the *padāṁśas; parikīrtitāḥ—*described as; *racanā—*the style of composition; *ca—*and; *tridhā—*threefold; *dīrgha—*large number; *madhya—*medium amount; *rikta—*devoid; *samāsataḥ—*in consideration of compounds.

**The following are** ***padāṁśas: prakṛti, pratyaya, kāla, vartamānādi, sambandha, vacana, puruṣa-vyatyaya, taddhita, upasarga, nipāta, sarvanāma, karma-bhūtādhikaraṇa, avyayī-bhāva,*** **and** ***pūrva-nipāta.*** **Additionally, there are three styles of composition: i) much usage of compounds, ii) medium usage of compounds, and iii) no usage of compounds.**

*dīrgha-samāsā madhya-samāsāsamāsā cety arthaḥ. varṇā mṛda-kaṭhorādyās te paścāt pratipāditāḥ. ādya-śabdān madhurāḥ. eṣāṁ vyañjakatve diṅ-mātram udāharaṇam.*

In these verses, the word *dīrgha-madhya-rikta-samāsataḥ* means many compounds, some compounds, and no compounds. The *varṇas* are the soft one (*prasāda*), the hard ones (*ojas*), and the sweet ones (*mādhurya*). These *varṇas* will be discussed later on. Now some examples of *padāṁśas* being suggestive will be given.

### Text 52

*tatra prakṛter yathā—*

In that regard, an example where a *prakṛti* is suggestive:

***mā kuru mānini mānaṁ suhṛdāṁ vacanaṁ praveśaya śravaṇe***  
***gokula-mahendra-tanayo bhavatu sa-nāthaḥ prasādam āsādya***

*mā kuru—*don’t do; *mānini—*O sulky girl; *mānam—*loving anger; *suhṛdām—*of the friends; *vacanam—*the words; *praveśaya—*make enter; *śravaṇe—*in your ears; *gokula-mahā-indra—*of the king of Gokula; *tanayaḥ—*the son; *bhavatu—*may He become; *sa-nāthaḥ—*one who has a master (You); *prasādam—*\[Your\] mercy; *āsādya—*having obtained.

\[A *gopī* says to Śrīmatī Rādhikā:\]

**O sulky girl, don’t be in** ***māna*****! Listen to your friends’ advice. Having secured Your mercy, let the son of the king of Gokula become Your obedient servant.**

*atra kṛñ-prakṛtyā kṛter aicchikatvam, tena tan-māna-karaṇaṁ tvad-iṣṭādhīnam eva, na tu sāhajikatvam, tasyāparādhābhāvāt, tena vibhāvanālaṅkāraḥ, tasmān mā kuru, tyajety arthaḥ. suhṛd-vacanaṁ tava śravaṇe praviśad api na praveṣṭum śaknoti, tvan-manasaḥ śravaṇena sahāsaṁyogāt. tat praveśaya, mano dīyatām. suhṛt-sambandhitvena vacanasya śravaṇa-praveśo yukta eveti ṇic-pratyaya-dhvaniḥ.*

Here, because of the base *\[ḍu\]kṛ\[ñ\]* (in *kuru*), it is understood that the activity is done due to desire. Therefore, “Your producing *māna* is based on Your desire only; the *māna* isn’t arising by itself since Kṛṣṇa hasn’t committed any offense.” In this way there is the *alaṅkāra* called *vibhāvanā* (“an effect without a normal cause”). Therefore the words “Don’t be in *māna*” mean “Give up your *māna*.”

“Even though the words of Your friends seemingly enter Your ears they are unable to penetrateas Your mind isn’t connected to Your ears. Therefore allow the words to enter. Surrender you mind. It’s Your friends’ advice, therefore it’s proper that it should enter Your ears.” This suggestion arises on account of the *pratyaya \[ṇ\]i* (in *praveśaya*).

### Text 53

*pratyayasya yathā—*

This verse is an instance where a *pratyaya* is suggestive:

***Prākṛta:***  
***āṇia bha-aṇa-duāraṁ dharaṇīe pāiāni kusumāiṁ***  
***pia-sahi kiṁ iti vasīdasi puṇo vi cala tattha kumumatthaṁ***

***Saṁskṛta:***  
***ānīya bhavana-dvāraṁ dharaṇyāṁ pātitāni kusumāni***  
***priya-sakhi kim iti viṣīdasi punar api cala tatra kusumārtham***

*ānīya—*having brought; *bhavana—*of the house; *dvāram—*to the door; *dharaṇyām—*on the ground; *pātitāni—*were made to fall; *kusumāni—*the flowers; *priya-sakhi—*O dear friend; *kim iti—*why; *viṣīdasi—*are you so depressed; *punar api—*once again; *cala—*go; *tatra—*there; *kusuma—*\[collecting\] flowers; *artham—*for the purpose of.

**After bringing the flowers to the entrance of the house, you made them fall on the ground. Dear friend! Why are you despondent? Go there again to collect flowers.**

*atra kusumāharaṇa-cchalena vṛndāvanaṁ gatvā saṅketa-sthale kṛṣṇam āgataṁ vīkṣya kusumāny ādāyaiva gṛham āgatāṁ punaḥ saṅketa-muralī-svana-śravaṇānantaraṁ punar-gamanotkaṇṭhayā vyājena pātita-kusumāṁ kāñcit prati hṛdaya-jñā sakhī vadati—priya-sakhi, kim iti viṣīdasi bhūmi-pātitāni kusumāni deva-deyāni na bhavanti, punar api tatra kusumārthaṁ gaccha. mayā te guru-jano bodhanīyaḥ, na kiñcid api te bhayam iti ṇic-pratyayasyaiva dhvaniḥ.*

A certain *gopī,* having gone to Vṛndāvana with the excuse of collecting flowers, having seen Kṛṣṇa who had come to the appointed meeting place, and having taken flowers, comes back to the house. Then, again hearing the sound of the flute, which was indicating a message, she makes the flowers fall because of an eagerness to go out again. Seeing this, a *sakhī,* understanding her heart, says: “O dear friend! Why are you despondent? Flowers that have fallen on the ground can’t be offered to the deity. Go there again to collect flowers. I shall explain everything to your elders. Don’t be afraid.” This suggestion arises due to the *pratyaya \[ṇ\]i* (in *pātitāni*).

## Atha śabdālaṅkāra-nirṇayaḥ.

Now we begin a discussion on the *śabdālaṅkāras* (“the ornaments of sound”).

### Text 1-2

(1-2) *atha (prathama-kiraṇe prathama-kārikāyām (1.5)) “upamiti-mukho ’laṅkṛti-gaṇaḥ” iti yad uktaṁ tatra mukha-śabdasya mukhyārthatvād amukhyasya prāptau prathamato ’mukhyaṁ śabdālaṅkāram evāha—*

In the first chapter (1.5) it was said, *upamiti-mukho ’laṅkṛti-gaṇaḥ:* “Simile and so on are the ornaments \[of the *kāvya-puruṣa*\].” In that regard, because the word *mukha* means “foremost,” it follows that there is a secondary topic. Thus, at first, the author elaborates on the secondary ornaments, the *śabdālaṅkāras.* \[The *arthālaṅkāras* are described in the next chapter.\]

***ekenārthena yat proktam anyenārthena cānyathākriyate śleṣa-kākubhyāṁ sā vakroktir bhaved dvidhā***  
***śleṣo ’pi ca bhaved dvedhā sa-bhaṅgābhaṅga-bhedataḥ***

*ekena arthena—*with one meaning; *yat—*which word; *proktam—*is spoken; *anyena arthena—*with another meaning; *ca—*and; *anyathā—*in another way; *kriyate—*is made; *śleṣa-kākubhyām—*because of a double meaning, or due to the intonation; *sā—*that; *vakra-uktiḥ—*called vakrokti (“crooked speech”); *bhavet—*is; *dvidhā—*twofold; *śleṣaḥ—*double meaning; *api—*also; *ca—*and; *bhavet—*is; *dvedhā—*twofold; *sa-bhaṅga—*with breaking up (of the words in a different manner); *abhaṅga—*without breaking up (the word); *bhedataḥ—*according to the varieties.

**When a word is spoken in one meaning \[by** ***abhidhā vṛtti*****\] but construed in another by** ***śleṣa*** **(“double meaning”) or** ***kāku*** **(“intonation”), such crooked speech is called** ***vakrokti śleṣa*** **is also twofold:*****abhaṅga*** **(where the double meaning is attained without breaking up the word in a different way, i.e the word itself has two meanings) and** ***sa-bhaṅga*** **(where the double meaning is attained by breaking up the word in a different way)..**

*śleṣeṇa kākvā vā śleṣaś ca dvidhā—abhaṅgaḥ sa-bhaṅgaś ceti*

*Vakrokti* is twofold on account of *śleṣa* or *kāku*, and *śleṣa* is also twofold: *abhaṅga* and *sa-bhaṅga.*

### Text 3

(3) *atrābhāṅga-śleṣeṇa yathā—*

In this regard, an example of *vakrokti* that is accomplished by *abhaṅga-śleṣa* is:

***kas tvaṁ śyāma harir babhūva tad idaṁ vṛndāvanaṁ nirmṛgaṁ***  
***haṁho nāgari mādhavo ’smy asamaye vaiśākha-māsaḥ kutaḥ***  
***mugdhe viddhi janārdano ’smi tad iyaṁ yogyā vane ’vasthitir***  
***bāle ’haṁ madhusūdano ’smi viditaṁ yogyo dvi-repho bhavān***

*kaḥ tvam—*who are you?; *śyāma—*O Śyāma; *hariḥ—*Hari; *babhūva—*became; *tad—*because of that; *idam—*this; *vṛndāvanam—*Vṛndāvana; *nirmṛgam—*(a place) from which the deers have gone; *haṁho—*(an interjection used for a jovial dramatic effect); *nāgari—*O lover; *mādhavaḥ—*Mādhava; *asmi—* I am; *asamaye—*at the wrong time; *vaiśākha-māsaḥ—*the month of Vaiśākha (April-May); *kutaḥ—*how; *mugdhe—*O bewildered girl; *viddhi—*know; *janārdanaḥ—*Janārdana; *asmi—*I am; *tad—*therefore; *iyam—*this; *yogyā—*is proper; *vane—*in the forest; *avasthitiḥ—*the abiding; *bāle—*hey girl; *aham*—I; *madhusūdanaḥ—*Madhusūdana; *asmi—*am; *viditam—*it is known; *yogyaḥ—*it is proper; *dvi-rephaḥ—*(one who has) two r’s; *bhavān—*Sir.

**Śrī Rādhikā said: Hey Śyāma! Who are You?**

**Śrī Kṛṣṇa replied: I am Hari (or: “I am a lion”).**

**Rādhikā: Ah! That’s why all the deers have left Vṛndāvana.**

**Kṛṣṇa: Well excuse Me, amorous girl! I am Mādhava (or: “the month of Vaiśakha”).**

**Rādhikā: Oh, how is it that the month of Vaiśākha has come at the wrong time?**

**Kṛṣṇa: Silly girl! Know that I am Janārdana (or: “one who gives pain to the living entities, i.e a dacoit or a hunter).**

**Rādhikā: In that case Your residing in the forest is befitting.**

**Kṛṣṇa: Dear girl! I am Madhusūdana (or: “I am a bee (*****bhramara*****)”).**

**Rādhikā: I know! It is proper that Your good self should have two r’s.**

### Text 4

(4) *sa-bhaṅgo yathā—*

An example of *vakrokti* that is accomplished by *sabhaṅga-śleṣa* is:

***kānte kīrtir akīrtir eva vada me kiñcij jaḍebhyaḥ paraṁ***  
***dhīrā kāpi bhavaty aho katham aho buddhir bhavet pūrṇimā***  
***kā medhā tava bhūyasī na madane tvayy eva me dhāraṇaṁ***  
***tan mām āspṛśa na spṛśeyam iti sa śrīmān jito rādhayā***

*kānte—*O lover; *kīrtiḥ—*fame; *akīrtiḥ—*infamy; *eva—*only; *vada me—*tell me; *kiñcit—*something; *jaḍebhyaḥ—*than inert things; *param—*other; *dhīrā—*an intelligent woman; *kā—*what; *api—*also; *bhavati—*is; *aho—*how amazing; *katham—*why; *aho—*(the exclamation *aho*); *buddhiḥ—*intelligence; *bhavet—*is; *pūrṇimā—*the full moon; *kā—*what; *medhā—*intelligence; *tava—*your; *bhūyasī—*is; *na—*not; *madane—*on Cupid; *tvayi—*on you; *eva—*only; *me—*my; *dhāraṇam—*mental focus; *tat—*therefore; *mām—*me; *āspṛśa—*please touch softly; *na spṛśeyam—*untouchable; *iti—*thus; *saḥ—*that famous; *śrīmān—*beautiful and glorious; *jitaḥ—*is defeated; *rādhayā—*by Rādhā.

Or, by double meaning (following Viśvanātha’s commentary):***kānte***—*kā ante* (meaning *kā ante nāśe sati tiṣṭhati?*): what remains at the end of life?; ***kiñcid***—*kiṁ cid* (meaning *kiṁ cid-vastu?*): what is a transcendental object?; ***dhīḥ***—intelligence; ***rākāpi***—*rākā api:* the full moon also; ***kāme***—on Cupid; ***dhā***—(meaning *dhāraṇam* (*dhā* + *\[k\]vi\[p\]*)): fixing the mind; ***mā mā spṛśa***—don’t touch me.

**Kṛṣṇa: My dear lover…**

**Rādhikā (thinking He said, “What remains in the end?”): Only fame and infamy remain.**

**Kṛṣṇa: Tell Me something!**

**Rādhikā (thinking He said, “Tell me what is spirit*****?*****”): It’s different than inert things.**

**Kṛṣṇa: Oh! Someone here is intelligent!**

**Rādhikā (thinking He said, “Oh! Intelligence is a full moon”” ): “How is intelligence a full moon?”**

**Kṛṣṇa: What is your intelligence then?!**

**Rādhikā (thinking He said, “Your attention is on Cupid”): My attention is on You only, not on Cupid.**

**Kṛṣṇa: Well in that case You can touch Me.**

**Rādhikā (thinking He said, “Don’t touch me”): You are untouchable.**

**In this way Rādhikā defeated Kṛṣṇa.**

### Text 5

(5) *kākvā yathā—*

Now an example is shown where there is ambiguity due to *kāku:*

***na vadasi hariṇāpi pṛcchyamānā***  
***na bata vilokayase vilokyamānā***  
***nijam abhimatam īhyatām idānīṁ***  
***vidhu-vadane samayaḥ sa no na bhāvī***

*na vadasi—*you do not speak; *hariṇā—*by Hari; *api—*although; *pṛcchyamānā—*being asked; *na—*don’t; *bata—*alas; *vilokayase—*you speak; *vilokyamānā—*being seen; *nijam—*personal; *abhimatam—*desire; *īhyatām—*should be acted upon; *idānīm—*now; *vidhu-vadane—*O moon-face girl; *samayaḥ—*time; *saḥ—*that; *naḥ—*our; *na bhāvī—*will not happen (or: will it not happen?).

**\[A** ***sakhī*** **says to Rādhikā:\] O moon-face girl! Although Hari inquires from You, You don’t say anything. Alas! He looks at You but You don’t even glance at Him. Now compose yourself. The time \[for hearing the song of the flute\]will not come.**

**(Or: Will the time not come?)**

*api tu bhāvy eva, yadā tu bhaviṣyati, tadā vaikalyaṁ ca drakṣyāma iti bhāvaḥ.*

The meaning is: It will certainly come. And when it does, we *sakhīs* shall see Your excitement.

\[See the appendix at the end of this chapter for examples from *Gopāla-campū* of *sa-bhaṅga-śleṣa* and *kāku.*\]

### Text 6

*(6) anuprāsyata ity arthe ’nuprāso varṇa-sāmyataḥ. sajātīyaṁ varṇam anu sajātīya-varṇāntaraṁ prakarṣeṇāsyata iti vyutpattiḥ.*

*Anuprāsa* (“alliteration”) occurs on account of repetition of the same *varṇa.* The meaning of *anuprāsa* is *anuprāsyate.* Etymologically it means: “ Preeminently placing a *varṇa* after another *varṇa* of the same kind.”

### Text 7

*sa ca dvedhā cheka-vṛtti-bhedāt (7)*

***Anuprāsa*** **is twofold:** ***cheka*** **and** ***vṛtti.***

*chekānuprāso vṛtty-anuprāsaś ceti sa dvedhā.*

The two kinds of *anuprāsa* are *chekānuprāsa* and *vṛtty-anuprāsa.*

### Text 8

*chekaḥ* ***sakṛttayā*** *(8)*

***Cheka*** **involves an immediate repetition .**

*eka-vārānu nyāsena chekaḥ syāt.*

*Cheka*is characterized as placing a *varṇa* only once immediately after a *varṇa* of the same kind.

### Text 9

(9) *yathā—*

An example of *chekānuprāsa:*

***dhāma śyāmam idaṁ śrī-daṁ***  
***jagato ’viratodayam***  
***dhyeyaṁ geyaṁ ca sarveṣāṁ***  
***dṛśoḥ prema yaśo-mayam***

*dhāma—*body; *śyāmam—*dark blue; *idam—*this; *śrī-dam—*giver of beauty; *jagataḥ—*to the world; *avirata-udayam—*continually appearing; *dhyeyam—*should be meditated upon; *geyam—*should be glorified; *ca—*and; *sarveṣām—*of everyone; *dṛśoḥ—*of the eyes; *prema—*the abode of love; *yaśo-mayam—*the embodiment of fame.

**This dark blue body \[which is known as Kṛṣṇa\] should be meditated upon and extolled. It is continually appearing and it gives beauty to the world. It is the embodiment of fame, and the abode of love for everyone’s eyes.**

*atra ma-kārādīnāṁ sakṛd anu nyāsaś ca.*

In this example, there is an instance of *ma-kāra* being placed immediately one after another (*śyāmam*), and similarly with *ya-rāma* (*dhyeyam*).

### Text 10

*mādhurya-vyañjakatvena sa eva hy upanāgaraḥ (10)*

**The same** ***chekānuprāsa*** **is called** ***upanāgara*** **when it suggests sweetness.**

*sa eva varṇa-vinyāsaḥ.*

That also involves repeating a *varṇa* only.

### Text 11

(*11*) *yathā—*

An example of *upanāgara* is shown:

***anaṅga-maṅgalārambhe***  
***sambhedaḥ sveda-kampayoḥ***  
***śaṅke paṅke-ruha-dṛśo***  
***na rasyānanda-mattatā***

*anaṅga—*(of the pastimes) of the god of love; *maṅgala-ārambhe—*in the happy beginning; *sambhedaḥ—*a combination; *sveda-kampayoḥ—*of perspiring and trembling; *śaṅke—*I fear; *paṅke-ruha-dṛśaḥ—*of one who has eyes like a lotus (“growing in the mud”); *na—*not; *rasya—*delightful; *ānanda-mattatā—*the state of being exhilarated due to bliss.

**\[One** ***sakhī,*** **seeing through a window her** ***yūtheśvarī*** **enjoying with Kṛṣṇa, tells another** ***sakhī:*****\] This lotus-eyed girl is already experiencing perspiring and trembling right at the beginning of the union. I fear she will not become fully intoxicated with passion \[i.e the union will not become complete due to the obstacles created by the perspiring and trembling\].**

### Text 12

*ekasyāty athavānekasyāmreḍitatayā yadi nyāsaḥ syād vṛtty-anuprāsaḥ (12)*

***Vṛtty-anuprāsa*** **occurs if one or more** ***varṇas*** **is repeated one or more times.**

### Text 13

(13) *yathā—*

An example of *vṛtty-anuprāsa* is shown \[where there is multiple repetition of a single *varṇa*\]:

***dhāma śyāmalam uddāma***  
***kāma-koṭi-manoharam***  
***dhyeyaṁ geyaṁ samāstheyaṁ***  
***samāneyaṁ ca mānase***

*dhāma—*body; *śyāmalam—*dark blue; *uddāma—*unlimited, or very powerful; *kāma-koṭi-manoharam—*which is more attractive than millions of Cupids; ; *dhyeyam—*should be meditated upon; *geyam—*should be glorified; *samāstheyam—*should be taken shelter of; *samāneyam—*it should be brought; *ca—*and; *mānase—*in the mind.

**Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s body is dark blue and very powerful. It is more attractive than millions of Cupids. It must be meditated upon, glorified, completely resorted to, and brought within one’s mind.**

### Text 14

(14) *anekasya yathā—*

Now an example of *vṛtty-anuprāsa* is given where there is a multiple repetition of more than one *varṇa:*

***vividha-vadhū-vadha-sādho virama ramā-nātha nāthyatāṁ nātha***  
***kaitava-vijña samajñāṁ tava vijñātuṁ na vijñāham***

*vividha—*many; *vadhū—*young wives; *vadha—*at killing; *sādhu—*O expert; *virama—*please stop; *ramā-nātha—*O Lord of Ramā,; *nāthyatām—*asking should be done; *na—*not; *atha—*then; *kaitava—*of deceitfulness; *vijña—*O knower; *samajñām—*fame; *tava—*your; *vijñātum—*to understand; *na—*not; *vijñā—*intelligent; *aham—*I am.

**\[A** ***gopī*** **says to Kṛṣṇa:\] O expert at killing young wives! Stop, O Lord of Ramā, and don’t later ask me for anything. O great deceiver! I am not intelligent enough to understand the extent of Your glories.**

### Text 15-16

(15-16) *eṣa ca dvividho bhavet, mādhuryaujo ’nukūlatvāt.*

*Vṛtty-anuprāsa* is *twofold* on account of being favorable either to *mādhurya* (“sweetness”) or to *ojas* (“vigor”).

### Text 17

(17) *krameṇodāharaṇe—*

Examples are given in order. First, *vṛtty-anuprāsa* that is favorable to *mādhurya* :

***anaṅga-saṅgarāsaṅge***  
***bhaṅgimeva sa jaṅgamaḥ***  
***saṅgīta-raṅgī tanv-aṅgī-***  
***saṅgī rāsaṅ gato hariḥ***

*anaṅga—*of Cupid; *saṅgara—*with the battle; *āsaṅge—*in connection; *bhaṅgimā—*the state of undulating; *iva—*like; *saḥ—*that famous (Hari); *jaṅgamaḥ—*moving living entity, or: who moves crookedly; *saṅgīta-raṅgī—*who enjoys music; *tanu-aṅgī—*of girls whose bodies are fine; *saṅgī—*who has the association; *rāsam gataḥ—*who has attained the *rāsa* dance; *hariḥ—*Hari.

**During the battle of Cupid, Hari moves like a wave. Now He has gone to the** ***rāsa*** **dance where He enjoys music and the company of slender girls.**

### Text 18

(18) Second, *vṛtty-anuprāsa* that is favorable to *ojas*:

***uddaṇḍa-kāma-kaṇḍūla-***  
***bāhu-maṇḍala-caṇḍimā***  
***śrīkhaṇḍa-piṇḍa-hiṇḍīra-***  
***puṇḍarīka-yaśā hariḥ***

*uddaṇḍa—*extraordinary; *kāma—*during love; *kaṇḍūla—*(is specialized in) the scratching; *bāhu-maṇḍala—*of whose arms; *caṇḍimā—*the intense power; *śrīkhaṇḍa—*sandalwood; *piṇḍa—*a ball of rice; *hiṇḍīra—*foam; *puṇḍarīka-yaśā—*whose fame is spotless like a white lotus; *hariḥ—*Hari.

**Hari, whose arms show their special power during the grand scratching in the battle of love, enjoys unblemished fame, which is pure like the whiteness of** ***śrīkhaṇḍa*** **sandalwood, a sacrificial rice ball, foam, or a white lotus.**

### Text 19

(19) *ca-kārāt padāvayava-jo ’pi ca. yathā—*

Due to the use of the word *ca* \[in text 7.15\], *vṛtty-anuprāsa* also occurs when there is repetition of a portion of a word. For example:

***uddāma-mādhavī-dāma-***  
***kaṇṭha utkaṇṭhayā hariḥ***  
***rādhāṁ nātidurārādhāṁ***  
***sasāra rasa-sāra-vit***

*uddāma—*extraordinary; *mādhavī—*of *mādhavī* flowers (“spring flowers”); *dāma—*a garland; *kaṇṭhaḥ—*on whose neck; *utkaṇṭhayā—*due to longing; *hariḥ—*Hari; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *na atidurārādhām—*worshiping whom is not extremely difficult; *sasāra—*followed; *rasa-sāra-vit—*who knows the essence of *rasa.*

**Hari knows the essence of** ***rasa.*** **Sporting a lovely** ***mādhavī*** **flower garland on His neck, out of longing He followed Rādhā who is not exceedingly difficult to worship.**

### Text 20

(20) *evam ojasyāpi “ṣaṣṭha-kiraṇe 29 ślokaḥ” ūrdhvordhvaṁ sarva-mūrdhnā” ity-ādi.*

The verse beginning *ūrdhvordhvam* (6.29) also belongs to the category of *ojas-vṛtty-anuprāsa.*

***ūrdhvordhvaṁ sarva-mūrdhnāṁ mukuṭa-taṭa-luṭhad-ratna-dīpti-cchaṭābhiś***  
***channaṁ yat-pāda-pīṭhaṁ prakaṭita-paṭima-prauḍhi-garbhair mahobhiḥ***  
***śarvāder garva-kharvī-karaṇa-cala-bhujā-daṇḍa-śauṭīrya-caṇḍaḥ***  
***śiṣṭābhīṣṭaṁ kṛṣīṣṭa pracura-ghana-ghṛṇā-vikrama-cakrapāṇiḥ***

*ūrdhva-ūrdhvam—*is placed atop; *sarva—*of all \[the demigods and kings\]; *mūrdhnām—*the heads; *mukuṭa—*of the crown; *taṭa—*on the rim; *luṭhat—*dangling; *ratna—*of the jewels; *dīpti—*radiance; *chaṭābhiḥ—*by the profuse; *channam—*covered; *yat—*whose; *pāda-pīṭham—*footstool; *prakaṭita—*manifest (i.e evident); *paṭima—*cleverness; *prauḍhi—*and boldness; *garbhaiḥ—*within which; *mahobhiḥ—*with the glories; *śarva—*of Śiva (“He who kills with arrows”); *ādeḥ—*and others; *garva—*the pride; *kharvī-karaṇa—*which make small; *cala—*moving; *bhujā—*arms; *daṇḍa—*of the polelike; *śauṭīrya—*on account of the prowess; *caṇḍaḥ—*is fierce; *śiṣṭa—*of the learned; *abhīṣṭam—*the desires; *kṛṣīṣṭa—*may He fulfill; *pracura—*(which is like) many; *ghana—*clouds; *ghṛṇā—*is compassion; *vikrama—*whose course of action; *cakra-pāṇiḥ—*Kṛṣṇa (“In whose hand there is a *cakra*”).

**May the holder of the** ***cakra*** **fulfill the desires of the learned. All the demigods and kings place his footstool atop their heads and thus his footstool is covered**

**by the profuse effulgence of the jewels on their crowns. By nature He is compassionate like many rain-giving clouds, yet He is also fierce on account of His swift and powerful polelike arms, which diminish the pride of Śiva and others with their glories that are replete with cleverness and boldness.**

*komalo lāṭa iṣyate komala-varṇānuprāso lāṭānuprāsaḥ*

**The word** ***lāṭa*** **means “soft and pleasing.” A** ***lāṭānuprāsa*** **is an alliteration of a soft** ***varṇa.***

*udāharaṇam—(ṣaṣṭha kiraṇe tṛtīyaḥ ślokaḥ) “līlālasa-lalitāṅgī laghu laghu lalanā-lalāma mauli-maṇiḥ” ity-ādi.*

For example:

***līlālasa-lalitāṅgī laghu laghu lalanā-lalāma-mauli-maṇiḥ***  
***lalitādibhir ālibhir vilasati lalita-smita-rādhā (6.3)***

*līlā—*due to pastimes; *ālasa—*is tired; *lalita-aṅgī—*whose charming body; *laghu laghu—*lightly, yet actively; *lalanā—*of women; *lalāma—*among the best; *mauli-maṇiḥ—*who is the crest jewel; *lalitā-ādibhiḥ—*beginning with Lalitā; *ālibhiḥ—*with the lady friends; *vilasati—*plays; *lalita—*lovely; *smita—*smile; *rādhā—*Rādhā.

**Śrī Rādhā has a lovely smile and She is the foremost of leading ladies. Since Her charming body is now somewhat tired due to play, She enjoys light recreation with Lalitā and other friends.**

*eṣa kaiścic chithila ity ucyate*

**Some scholars call such alliteration** ***śithila*** **(“relaxed”).**

### Text 21

*tātparya-mātra-bhede syāl lāṭa ity ucyate ’paraiḥ (21)*

**Others say** ***lāṭānuprāsa*** **involves a difference only in the purpose of the repetition.**

*śabdārthayor abhede ’pi tātparya-mātra-bhinnatve lāṭo vidagdhas tasya priyatvāt lāṭānuprāsa ity aparair ucyate.*

Although the words repeated are the same and their meaning is the same, the difference between those same words lies merely in the purpose of the repetition. Thus others describe *lāṭānuprāsa* in that way because its usage requires cleverness and because *lāṭa* is esteemed in *kāvya.*

### Text 22

*padasyāpy eṣaḥ (22)*

**This type of** ***lāṭānuprāsa*** **involves the repetition of a word.**

*eṣa lāṭānuprāsaḥ. padasyāpi, api-śabdāt padāṁśānāṁ padānām api.*

The word *eṣaḥ* in the text above means *lāṭānuprāsaḥ.* Due to the use of the word *api* (“also”) in *padasyāpi,* it is understood that there can also be, i) a repetition of a portion of a word; and ii) a repetition of many words.

### Text 23

(23) *krameṇodāharaṇāni—*

Now examples are given in order. \[This verse is an instance of a repetition of a word.\]

***kṛṣṇatvena sakhi prādurbhāvo manye manojaneḥ***  
***manojaneḥ punar iyad vaidagdhī-vaiduṣī kutaḥ***

*kṛṣṇatvena—*as Kṛṣṇa; *sakhi—*O lady friend; *prādurbhāvaḥ—*manifesting; *manye—*I think; *manojaneḥ—*Cupid (“He whose origin is the mind”); *manojaneḥ—*of Cupid; *punar—*moreover; *iyat—*such; *vaidagdhī—*in cleverness; *vaiduṣī—*skill; *kutaḥ—*how.

**Dear friend! I think Cupid has manifest as Kṛṣṇa. Then again, how is Cupid capable of such ingenuity?**

### Text 24

(24) \[This verse shows a repetition of a portion of a word:\]

***ratnāni ratnākara eva santi***  
***puṣpāni puṣpākara eva dhatte***  
***guṇo guṇa-jñe labhate prakāśaṁ***  
***yaśo yaśodā-suta-sevayaiva***

*ratnāni—*jewels; *ratnākare—*in the ocean; *eva—*only; *santi—*are; *puṣpāni—*flowers; *puṣpākare—*in a garden or a nursery; *eva—*only; *dhatte—*one plants; *guṇaḥ—*a quality; *guṇa-jñe—*in a good person; *prakāśam labhate—*becomes manifest; *yaśaḥ—*fame; *yaśodā-suta-sevayā—*by serving the son of Yaśodā; *eva—*exclusively.

**Jewels are found in a mine; flowers are planted in a garden; qualities are manifest in a good person; and fame occurs by serving Yaśodā’s son.**

### Text 25

(25) *padānāṁ yathā—*

Now an illustration where many words are repeated:

***yeṣāṁ na vṛndāvana-candra-līlā***  
***hṛdy asti teṣām aparair guṇaiḥ kim***  
***yeṣāṁ tu vṛndāvana-candra-līlā***  
***hṛdy asti teṣām aparair guṇaiḥ kim***

*yeṣām—*whose; *na—*not; *vṛndāvana-candra-līlā—*Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes; *hṛdi—*in the heart; *asti—*is; *teṣām—*their; *aparaiḥ—*of additional; *guṇaiḥ—*qualities; *kim—*what is the use; *yeṣām—*whose; *tu—*but; *vṛndāvana-candra-līlā—*Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes; *hṛdi—*in the heart; *asti—*is; *teṣām—*their; *aparaiḥ—*of additional; *guṇaiḥ—*qualities; *kim—*what is the use.

**What is the use of additional qualities for those in whose heart Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes don’t manifest? And for those in whose heart Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes do manifest, what is the use of additional qualities?**

### Text 26

*tatraiva vṛttāv anyatra vā punaḥ. vṛtty-avṛttyoś ca vā nāmnaḥ sārūpye syād athāparaḥ (26)*

**Another variety of** ***lāṭānuprāsa*** **occurs when there is repetition of a** ***nāma*** **in the same compound or outside of the compound.**

*tatraiva vā samāse ’nyatra vā ’samāse, samāsāsamāsayor vā, nāmna eva, na tu padasya, sārūpye-saty aparo lāṭānuprāsaḥ.*

In the passage above, the words *tatraiva vṛttāv anyatra vā* mean “in the same *samāsa* or not in a *samāsa.*” So another variety of *lāṭānuprāsa* occurs when there is repetition of just of a *nāma,* and not of a *pada,* in the same compound or outside of the compound.

### Text 27

(*27*) *udāharaṇam—*

For example:

***hima-kiraṇa-kiraṇa-madhurā***  
***rākā rākāmṛtāṁśu-mukha bhavataḥ***  
***virahe virahe mūrchā-***  
***sakhyās tāṁ kevalaṁ dahati***

*hima-kiraṇa—*of the moon; *kiraṇa-madhurā—*which is sweet on account of the rays; *rākā—*the day of Pūrṇimā; *rākā-amṛta-aṁśu-mukha—*O you whose face is like themoon on the day of Pūrṇimā; *bhavataḥ—*from you; *virahe—*in separation; *virahe—*in separation; *mūrchā-sakhyāḥ—*from her whose friend is unconsciousness; *tām—*her; *kevalam—*only; *dahati—*burns.

**\[A** ***gopī*** **tells Kṛṣṇa:\] Your face is like a full moon! Thus when you and this girl whose friend is unconsciousness are separated from each other, This full moon night, which is is sweet on account of the rays of the moon, burns only her.**

### Text 28

(28) *neha—*

But this is not the case in the following example. The following verse is included within the category of *yamaka* since the words that are repeated have different meanings.

***sumahima-hima-kara-madhurā madhu-rākāyāṁ niśā niśāteyaṁ***  
***tava rucira cira-vilambe pradahati tāṁ kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa-vartmeva***

*su-mahima—*whose glory is beautiful; *hima-kara—*on account of the moon; *madhurā—*charming; *madhu—*of spring; *rākāyām—*during the full moon; *niśā—*night; *niśātā—*sharp (*ni + śo* 4P *+ \[k\]ta + ā\[p\]*); *iyam—*this; *tava—*your; *rucira—*O beautiful one; *cira—*for a long time; *vilambe—*in regards to delaying; *pradahati—*scorches; *tām—*her; *kṛṣṇa—*O Kṛṣṇa; *kṛṣṇa-vartmā—*fire; *iva—*like.

**O beautiful Kṛṣṇa! Due to Your prolonged absence, this night of Vasanta** ***pūrṇimā*****, which is usually charming on account of the full moon, is sharp, and it burns her like fire.**

*idaṁ tu yamakāntargatam eva, bhinna-bhinnārthakatvāt. tad evaṁ varṇitaś cheka-vṛtti-padāvayavaika-pada-nāma-sārūpyāt ṣaṭ prakāro ’nuprāsaḥ.*

Now we summarize:

There are six kinds of *anuprāsa:* i) *cheka,* ii) *vṛtti,* iii) *lāṭānuprāsa* where there is a repetition of a word, iv) *lāṭānuprāsa* where there is a repetition of one part of a word, v) *lāṭānuprāsa* where there is a repetition of many words, and vi) *lāṭānuprāsa* where there is a repetition of a *nāma*.

### Text 29

***(29) yamakaṁ tv artha-bhinnānāṁ***  
***padādīnāṁ samākṛtiḥ***  
***kvacin nirarthakānāṁ ca***  
***sārthānarthavatāṁ kvacit***

***Yamaka*** **is the repetition of words and so on that have different meanings. Moreover, sometimes the words and so on are meaningless, and sometimes one is meaningful while the other is meaningless and vice versa.**

*parasparam artha-gata-bhedavatāṁ padādīnāṁ nāma-pada-padāvayava-vākyānāṁ samā ākṛtiḥ sārūpyam yamakam.*

The above passage means: The repetition (*samā ākṛtiḥ = sārupyam*) of a *nāma* (undeclined word), *pada* (declined word), part of a *pada*, or *vākya* (sentence) that has a different meaning when repeated is called *yamaka.*

### Text 30

*etac ca pāda-jatvena navadhā … (30)*

**Additionally, according to their appearance in specific lines of a verse,** ***yamakas*** **are ninefold.**

*etat yamakam.*

In the text above, the word *etat* means *yamakam.*

### Text 31-33

*… prathamasya tu*

***dvitīyena tṛtīyena caturtheneti tat tridhā***  
***dvitīyas tu tṛtīyena caturtheneti ca dvidhā***  
***tṛtīyas tu caturthenety eka eveti ṣaḍ bhidaḥ***  
***prathamas triṣv apīty anta iti sapta dvayaṁ punaḥ***  
***prathamas tu caturthena dvitīyas tat-pareṇa ca***  
***prathamas tu dvitīyena tṛtīyas tat-pareṇa ca***

**When an element of the first line of a verse is repeated in the second line, that makes one kind of** ***yamaka.*** **Similarly, the first line with the third, and the first with the fourth makes two more varieties, for a total of three kinds.**

**The second line with the third, and the second with the fourth makes two kinds. The third line with the fourth makes one kind, for a total of six varieties.**

**The first line repeated in the other three lines makes a total of seven. Now two more kinds are mentioned: The first line with the fourth as well as the second with the third; and the first line with the second as well as the third with the fourth.**

*prathamo dvitīyena tṛtīyena caturtheneti trayo bhedāḥ. dvitīyas tṛtīyena caturtheneti ca dvau. tṛtīyaś caturthenety ekaḥ. prathamas triṣv apīty ekaḥ. prathamaś caturthena dvitīyas tṛtīyenety ekaḥ. prathamo dvitīyena tṛtīyaś caturthenety ekaḥ—eva nava.*

This is the meaning of the three verses above: When \[an element in\] the first line of a verse is repeated either in the second line, in the third line, or in the fourth line, that makes one kind of *yamaka.* Thus three varieties of *yamakas* are counted so far. The second line in connection with the third line or the fourth line makes two varieties. The third line with the fourth line makes one kind. The first line repeated in all three other lines makes another kind. If the first line is repeated in the fourth line at the same time as the second line is repeated in the third, that is an additional variety. Finally, the first line with the second as well as the third line with the fourth makes one kind, for a total of nine varieties of *yamakas* \[which involve a connection between *pādas*\].

### Text 34

***ardha-śloka-ślokayoś cāvṛttyā dvedhā bhaved atha***  
***tenaikādaśa bhedāḥ syuḥ pāda-bhāge ca pūrva-vat***  
***navadheti bhidā jñeyā viṁśatir yamakodbhavāḥ (34)***

**By repeating half of a verse or by repeating a whole verse, two more varieties of** ***yamakas*** **are created, thus bringing the total to eleven. And when an element is repeated in the same line and that repetition is itself repeated in another line in one of the combinations mentioned before (see previous verse), then another nine varieties are created, for a total of twenty** ***yamakas.***

*ardha-ślokāvṛttyā ca punar ekādaśa. punar pādasya bhāge ca yad yamakaṁ tat pūrva-van navadheti viṁśatir bhedāḥ.*

By repeating a half verse (see 7.56), or a full verse (see 7.57-58), the total becomes eleven. Moreover, that *yamaka* which comes about when an element is repeated in the same line and that repetition is itself repeated in another line (see 7.47-55) is of nine kinds like before, and thus there are twenty varieties.

### Text 35

***pādasya tu tri-khaṇḍatve triṁśad bhedāḥ prakīrtitāḥ***  
***catuḥ-khaṇḍatve punaś catvāriṁśad bhaved bhidāḥ (35)***

**Moreover when three elements are repeated in the same line, the total becomes thirty varieties of** ***yamakas.*** **Furthermore, when four elements are repeated in the same line repeated, the total is forty. \[See 7.67.\]**

### Text 36

(36) *diṅ-mātraṁ udāhriyate—*

Now merely the direction is shown:

***sura-tarur eṣa natānāṁ***  
***surata-rucir gopa-taruṇīnām***  
***tri-bhuvana-jana-kamanīyo***  
***jayatād ābhīra-rāja-yuva-rājaḥ***

*sura-taruḥ—*desire tree; *eṣaḥ—*this; *natānām—*for the devotees (who are bowed down); *surata—*for pastimes of Eros; *ruciḥ—*who has longing; *gopa-taruṇīnām—*toward the young *gopīs; tri-bhuvana-jana—*by the people of the three worlds; *kamanīyaḥ—*who is desired; *jayatāt—*may he be glorious; *ābhīra—*of cowherds; *rāja—*of the king; *yuva-rājaḥ—*crown prince.

**May the dignified son of the king of cowherds be glorious. He is desired by everyone in the three worlds, He is a desire tree for the devotees, and He longs for intimacy with the** ***gopīs.***

### Text 37

***sura-sārtha-bhūṣita-padair brahmādibhir adhika-bhakti-sannamraiḥ***  
***surasārtha-bhūṣita-padaiḥ stavaiḥ stutaḥ keśi-hā jayati (37)***

*sura—*of demigods; *sārtha—*by a multitude; *bhūṣita—*are adorned; *padaiḥ—*whose two feet; *brahmā-ādibhiḥ—*by Brahmā and others; *adhika-bhakti—*with much devotion; *sannamraiḥ—*who are offering obeisances; *surasa—*liquid; *artha—*with substances; *bhūṣita—*are adorned; *padaiḥ—*whose feet; *stavaiḥ—*with hymns; *stutaḥ—*praised; *keśi-hā—*killer of Keśī; *jayati—*may he be glorious.

**All glories to the killer of Keśī. Although Brahmā and others have their feet pampered with delightful liquid substances and adorned by demigods, with much reverential devotion they praise Him with hymns.**

### Text 38

***man-mathana-namad-antarayā kuṭilo ’ñjana-kāla-kūṭāktaḥ***  
***priya-sakha kaṭākṣa-viśikho man-mathana-madaṁ sa rādhayā sasṛje (38)***

*man-mathana—*due to to amorous desire; *naman—*is stooped; *antarayā—*whose heart; *kuṭilaḥ—*crooked; *añjana—*eyeliner; *kāla-kūṭa—*poison; *aktaḥ—*smeared with; *priya-sakha—*O dear friend; *kaṭa-akṣa—*sidelong glance; *viśikhaḥ—*an arrow; *mad—*Me; *mathana—*in agitating; *madam—*so that there would be pride; *saḥ—*that famous; *rādhayā—*by Rādhā; *sasṛje—*ejected.

**O dear friend! Because Her heart is weighed down by amourous desire, Rādhā shot the arrow of Her crooked sidelong glance smeared with the poison of eyeliner just to exhibit her pride in being able to agitate Me.**

### Text 39

***jayati vraja-pati-tanayo namad-anamat-tulya-kāruṇyaḥ***  
***na madana mat-tulya-hatā kāpi vinā yena māṁ tudasi***

*jayati—*may he be glorious; *vraja-pati—*of the master of Vraja; *tanayaḥ—*the son; *namad—*toward those who are submissive; *anamat—*toward those who are not submissive; *tulya—*is indiscriminate, (or: *anamat-tulya* means “is very firm and unchangeable”); *kāruṇyaḥ—*whose compassion; *na—*no; *madana—*O Cupid; *mat-tulya—*equal to me; *hatā—*hurt (or greatly affected); *kā api—*someone; *vinā yena—*without whom; *mām—*me; *tudasi—*you strike.

**\[A** ***gopī*** **says:\] O Cupid! When the son of the master of Vraja is not near, you strike me! All glories to Him! He is dedicated to being very compassionate toward the meek, and no one is more smitten than I.**

### Text 40

***rādhā su-kumāra-tanur mad-anavadhānād upaiṣyati glānim***  
***baddho ’yam añjalis te madana vadhārthaṁ na tāṁ vitudeḥ***

*rādhā—*Rādhā; *su-kumāra—*is very delicate; *tanuḥ—*whosebody; *mad—*my; *anavadhānāt—*due to inattentiveness; *upaiṣyati—*will attain; *glānim—*exhaustion; *baddhaḥ—*joined; *ayam—*this; *añjaliḥ—*placing open hands side by side in reverence; *te—*to you; *madana—*O Cupid; *vadha-artham—*for the purpose of killing; *na—*not; *tām—*Her; *vitudeḥ—*you should not strike.

**O Cupid! Rādhā has such a delicate body. However, due to my negligence, She will become despondent. Folding my hands, I beg you: please don’t strike Her. If you do, She will die.**

### Text 41

***vraja-pati-nandana nandaya nandaya vṛṣabhānu-nandinī-hṛdayam***  
***madana-madojjvalam atulaṁ mada-namad-ojaḥ-prasāda-mādhuryam***

*vraja-pati-nandana—*O son of the king of Vraja; *nandaya nandaya—*please delight fully; *vṛṣabhānu-nandinī—*of the daughter of Vṛṣabhānu Mahārāja; *hṛdayam—*the heart; *madana—*of Cupid; *mada—*due to the intoxication; *ujjvalam—*which is burning; *atulam—*peerless; *mada—*due to pride; *namat—*bent, i.e crooked; *ojaḥ—*power; *prasāda—*serenity; *mādhuryam—*in which the sweetness.

**O son of the king of Vraja! Please completely delight the peerless heart of Vṛṣabhānu-nandinī. Her heart is burning in the fire of Cupid, and the power, serenity, and sweetness in her heart have become crooked due to pride.**

### Text 42

***kānanaṁ jayati yatra sadā sat kā na nandati yad etya sukha-śrīḥ***  
***kā na nanda-tanayasya manojñā kānanaṁ dhayati vā na hi tasya***

*kānanam—*may the forest \[of Vṛndāvana\]; *jayati—*be glorious; *yatra—*where; *sadā—*exists eternally; *sat—*the Absolute Truth; *kā—*who; *na nandati—*is not increased; *yat—*which (forest); *etya—*having attained; *sukha—*of happiness; *śrīḥ—*opulence; *kā—*which girl; *na—*not; *nanda—*of Nanda Mahārāja; *tanayasya—*to the son; *manojñā—*charming; *kā—*who; *ānanam—*the mouth; *dhayati—*drink; *vā—*indeed; *na hi—*does not; *tasya—*His.

**All glories to Vṛndāvana, where the Absolute Truth exists eternally! Which happiness doesn’t increase upon attaining Vṛndāvana? Is there any young** ***gopī*** **whom the son of Nanda doesn’t find charming? And is there any young** ***gopī*** **does not drink the honey of His lips?**

### Text 43

***yathā vā—***  
***nava-payodhara-kānta viramyatāṁ na vapa yodha-rasaṁ hṛdi mānmatham***  
***nava-payodhara-kānta sa vai bhavān nava-payo ’dhara-paḥ prati-yoṣitām***

*nava—*new; *payaḥ-dhara—*like that of a cloud (“that which holds water”); *kānta—*O You whose complexion; *viramyatām—*stop; *na vapa—*don’t sow; *yodha—*of a fighter; *rasam—*the mood; *hṛdi—*in the heart; *mānmatham—*related to Cupid (“who churns the mind”); *nava—*newly-grown; *payaḥ-dhara—*has breasts;; *kānta—*O You whose beloved; *saḥ—*He; *vai—*certainly; *bhavān—*is Your good self; *nava—*of young girls; *payaḥ—*who dries up the life airs; *adhara—*the lips; *paḥ—*who drinks, ; *prati-yoṣitām—*of rival ladies.

**O lover with the complexion of a new rain cloud! Please rest a while. Don’t sow in my heart a desire for an amorous battle. O You whose lover has newly-grown breasts! You dry up the life airs of young girls like me by drinking the lips of my rivals.**

### Text 44

***kāmaṁ dadhānā hṛdaye ’nurāgaṁ smarālasāṅgī vidunoti bālā***  
***smarālasāṅgī-kurutāṁ mukunda kā manda-dhānā na bhaved vinā tvām***

*kāmaṁ—*at will; *dadhānā—*who bears; *hṛdaye—*in the heart; *anurāgam—*love; *smara—*because of Cupid; *alasa—*is indolent; *aṅgī—*whose body; *vidunoti—*burns; *bālā—*the girl; *smara—*please remember; *alasa—*O lazy person; *aṅgī-kurutām—*please accept; *mukunda—*O you whose face is beautiful like a jasmine flower, or: O you who give liberation; *kā—*which girl; *manda—*is slow; *dhānā—*one whose attention; *na bhavet—*would not be; *vinā—*without; *tvām—*you.

**O Mukunda! This girl readily bears love for You in Her heart. But now Cupid has rendered Her indolent and She suffers in separation from You. Hey lazy fellow! At least remember Her! You should bring Her close to You. Which girl doesn’t lose her mind without You?**

### Text 45

***samasta-kalyāṇa-guṇaika-vāridhe samas tavāste katamas tri-lokyām***  
***namāmi me mādhava samprasīda na mām ime dyantu duranta-tāpāḥ***

*samasta—*of all; *kalyāṇa—*auspicious; *guṇa—*qualities; *eka-vāridhe—*O sole ocean; *samaḥ—*equal; *tava—*to you; *āste—*is; *katamaḥ—*who; *tri-lokyām—*in the three worlds; *namāmi—*I offer obeisances; *me—*with me; *mādhava—*O Mādhava; *samprasīda—*be pleased; *na—*not; *mām—*me; *ime—*these; *dyantu—*may they break to pieces; *duranta—*which have no end; *tāpāḥ—*afflictions.

**O sole ocean of all auspiciious qualities! I offer obeisances to You. Who is equal to You in the three worlds? O Mādhava, be pleased with me. Don’t let these never-ending afflictions break me to pieces.**

### Text 46

***yathā vā—***  
***sadā sad-ānanda-mayaṁ vapus te sa-dāsa-dāsī-nikaraṁ puraṁ ca***  
***maho mahoddāma-rasas tavāyam aho maho bhūri tavaiva kṛṣṇa***

*sadā—*always; *sat—*eternity; *ānanda—*bliss; *mayam—*composed of; *vapuḥ—*body; *te—*your; *sa—*existing with; *dāsa—*servants; *dāsī—*female servants; *nikaram—*many; *puram—*abode; *ca—*and; *mahaḥ—*festival; *mahā—*great; *uddāma—*unbounded; *rasaḥ—*delight; *tava—*your; *ayam—*this; *aho—*what a wonder; *mahaḥ—*glories; *bhūri—*abundant; *tava—*your; *eva—*only; *kṛṣṇa—*O Kṛṣṇa.

**O Kṛṣṇa! Only your glories are great. Your body is always made of eternity and bliss. Your abode is full of servants and maidservants. And Your festivalsYour festivals are a greatnt limitless delight.**

*pāda-jaṁ navadhā yamakaṁ darśitam.*

Nine kinds of *yamakas* arising in a *pāda* have been shown.

### Text 47

*atha pāda-bhāga-jāni darśyante—*

Now *yamakas*

where an element is repeated in the same line and that repetition is itself repeated in another line will be demonstrated (see 7.34).

***kala-haṁ kalahaṁsānāṁ kalahaṁ kala-haṁsakāḥ***  
***abhyasyantīva gopīnāṁ caraṇāmbhoja-vāsinaḥ***

*kala—*sweet sounds; *ham—*becomes, (or strikes); *kalahaṁsānām—*of geese; *kalaham—*a quarrel; *ka-la—*give happiness; *haṁsakāḥ—*anklets; *abhyasyanti—*are repeatedly performing; *iva—*like; *gopīnām—*of the *gopīs; caraṇa-ambhoja—*on the lotus feet; *vāsinaḥ—*staying.

**The delightful anklets staying on the** ***gopīs’*** **lotus feet seem to imitate the sweet-sounding quarrel of geese.**

### Text 48

***madhurā madhu-rākāyā rajanī sā yadājani***  
***madhu-rāma-dhurā kāsāṁ tadā nāsīd dhareḥ puraḥ***

*madhurā—*charming; *madhu—*of spring (Caitra or Vaiśākha); *rākāyāḥ—*of the full moon; *rajanī—*the night; *sā—*that; *yadā—*when; *ajani—*arose; *madhu—*on account of drinking honey wine; *rāma—*of joy; *dhurā—*an abundance; *kāsām—*among the girls; *tadā—*then; *na āsīt—*was there not; *hareḥ—*of Hari; *puraḥ—*in front.

**When the charming night of the full moon of Caitra came, which** ***gopī*** **inebriated by honey wine wasn’t ruttish in front of Hari?**

### Text 49

***sādhvasādhvany adattāṅghrir gurūṇām api sannidhau***  
***kṛṣṇaṁ vīkṣyotsukā sāsīt sādhv-asādhv-avicārataḥ***

*sādhvasa—*of fear; *adhvani—*on the path; *adatta—*were never offered (i.e put); *aṅghriḥ—*(a girl) by whom the feet; *gurūṇām—*of elders; *api—*even; *sannidhau—*in the presence of; *kṛṣṇam—*Kṛṣṇa; *vīkṣya—*having seen; *utsukā—*eager; *sā—*she; *āsīt—*was; *sādhu—*good; *asādhu—*improper; *avicārataḥ—*without considering.

**This girlnever walks on the path of fear. Seeing Kṛṣṇa, she becomes excited even in the presence of her elders, disregarding considerations of what is proper and what is improper.**

### Text 50

***kāñcī-dāmno ravas tasyā raṇato ’raṇato ’tanoḥ***  
***raṇa-toraṇato bhūri-śriṇaḥ kṛṣṇa-mano ’harat***

*kāñcī-dāmnaḥ—*of the belt; *ravaḥ—*the sound; *tasyāḥ—*her; *raṇataḥ—*tinkling; *araṇataḥ*because of the movement; *atanoḥ—*of Cupid (who doesn’t have a body); *raṇa—*of the battlefield; *toraṇataḥ—*than the the garland of leaves suspended across gateways; *bhūri—*is greater; *śriṇaḥ—*whose beauty; *kṛṣṇa—*of Kṛṣṇa; *manaḥ—*the mind; *aharat—*stole.

**The sound of her belt stole Kṛṣṇa’s mind, for her belt was more beautiful than the garland of leaves spread across the gateway of the battlefield of Cupid.**

### Text 51

***dṛśor agocareṇaiva***  
***hariṇā hariṇākṣi te***  
***kapola-bhittir mānebha-***  
***hariṇā hariṇākṛtiḥ***

*dṛśoḥ—*of sight; *agocareṇa—*being out of the range; *eva—* simply; *hariṇā—*by Hari’s; *hariṇa—*are like a doe’s; *akṣi—*O you whose eyes; *te—*your; *kapola—*of the cheeks; *bhittiḥ—*the surface; *māna—*of loving anger; *ibha—*for the elephant; *hariṇā—*(who is like) a lion; *hariṇa—*pale; *ākṛtiḥ—*appearance.

**O doe-eyed girl! Hari is a lion for the elephant of your pique. Simply by His being out of sight, your cheeks become pallid.**

### Text 52

***agocare sati harau dahaty eṣa mano mama***  
***madano mada-nodenāmadano mad-anojasā***

*agocare—*out of sight; *sati harau—*when Hari is; *dahati—*burns; *eṣaḥ—*this; *manaḥ—*heart; *mama—*of mine; *madanaḥ—*Cupid; *mada—*exhiliration; *nodena—*by means of removing; *amadanaḥ—*not happy; *mad—*my; *anojasā—*because of weakness.

**When Hari is out of sight, Cupid, being unpleased by my weakness, burns my heart by removing my exhiliration.**

### Text 53

***sa-dāsa-dāsī-nikaraiḥ paricchadaiḥ sadā-sad-ānanda-vilāsa-vigrahaḥ***  
***sa dāsa-dākṣiṇya-kṛpādibhir guṇaiḥ sad-āsa-dāro vilalāsa mādhavaḥ***

*sa-dāsa-dāsī-nikaraiḥ—*along with many male and female servants; *paricchadaiḥ—*along with paraphernalia; *sadā—*always; *sat—*eternity; *ānanda—*are composed of bliss; *vilāsa—*and pastimes; *vigrahaḥ—*whose form; *saḥ—*He; *dā—*liberality; *āsa—*splendor; *dākṣiṇya—*expertise; *kṛpā—*mercy; *ādibhiḥ—*beginning with; *guṇaiḥ—*with qualities; *sat—*is excellent; *āsa—*whose sitting pose; *dāraḥ—*whose wife; *vilalāsa—*looked beautiful; *mādhavaḥ—*Mādhava.

**Mādhava’s blissful form,pastimes, paraphernalia, servants, and maidservants are always made of eternity of bliss. His consort sitting resplendently next to Him, Heappears beautiful with His qualities such as charity, lustre, expertise, and mercy.**

### Text 54

***keśi nākeśi nāthena***  
***kāminākāmi nādaraḥ***  
***kāminā kāmi nā vā śrīḥ***  
***keśinā keśi-nāśinā***

*ka-īśi—*toward Varuṇa; *nāka-īśi—*toward Indra (the king of heaven); *nāthena—*by the Lord; *ka-aminā—*by Kṛṣṇa (who is very joyful); *akāmi—*was desired; *na—*not; *ādaraḥ—*respect; *kāminā—*who is lusty \[for the *gopīs*\]; *kā—*what; *āmi—*was obtained; *nā—*not; *vā—*indeed (particle of conjecture); *śrīḥ—*opulence; *keśinā—*by Kṛṣṇa (who has beautiful hair, or: who controls Brahmā (*ka*)); *keśi—*of Keśī; *nāśinā—*by the killer.

**Kṛṣṇa lusts for the** ***gopīs;*** **His hair is comely and He is always joyful. What opulence has not been obtained by the killer of Keśī? The Lord doesn’t even care to offer respects to Varuṇa or Indra.**

### Text 55

***ratyāviratyā vivṛtā navīnair***  
***atyāvir atyāvi manoja-rāgaiḥ***  
***rāse ’carāsecanakair vilāsair***  
***āse ca rāse caturā mṛgākṣī***

*ratyā—*out of affection; *aviratyā—*(an affection) without cessation; *vivṛtā—*surrounded in a special way; *navīnaiḥ—*by young men; *atyāviḥ—*in a very open way; *atyāvi—*was eminently protected; *manaḥ-ja—*has appeared in the heart; *rāgaiḥ—*whose love; *rāse—*in the *rāsa* dance; *acarā—*steadfast; *āsecanakaiḥ—*charming; *vilāsaiḥ—*on account of pastimes; *āse—*she appeared beautiful; *ca—*and; *rāse—*in regards to every single *rasa; caturā—*expert; *mṛga—*are like a doe’s; *akṣī—*whose eyes.

**The doe-eyed girl, who was fixated on the** ***rāsa*** **dancing and expert in all the rasas, appeared very beautiful on account of the charming pastimes.She was specially surrounded by loving young men, i.e many Kṛṣṇas, protecting her closely out of loyal affection.**

### Text 56

*evam aṣṭādaśa—*

In this way, eighteen types of *yamakas* have been shown so far.

***mano jahāra prati mā sa-mānā***  
***sarasvatī te mad-asu-svarūpe***  
***manoja-hāra-pratimā samānā***  
***sarasvatīte mada-susvarūpe***

*manaḥ—*mind; *jahāra—*stole; *prati—*toward; *mā—*me; *sa-mānā—*which is respectful; *sarasvatī—*words; *te—*your; *mad—*my; *asu—*of life airs; *svarūpe—*O embodiment; *manoja—*of Cupid (“who manifests in the heart”); *hāra—*of a necklace; *pratimā—*a symbol; *samānā—*straightforward; *sarasvati—*in the ocean; *ite—*O you who have entered; *mada—*of bliss; *su-svarūpe—*which is the embodiment.

**(Kṛṣṇa says to a beloved** ***gopī*****): O embodiment of my life airs, your words, which are so straightforward and respectful to me, have stolen my heart. Your words are like Cupid’s pearl necklace to me, O you have entered into the ocean of personified bliss.**

*ardha-vṛttiḥ.*

This verse is an example of a repetition of a half verse.

### Text 57

***na vaṁśī-karam āsādya***  
***yamānujāni-bhaṅgataḥ***  
***kasyā viśadatāṁ yāti***  
***mano māna-pariplavam***

*na—*does not; *vaṁśī—*of the flute; *karam—*the sweet sound; *āsādya—*having obtained (i.e having heard); *yama-anujāni—*that which arises after *yama* (rules, i.e do’s) \[in the eightfold *yoga* system\] i.e *niyama* (regulations, i.e don’t’s)”); *bhaṅgataḥ—*on account of breaking; *kasyāḥ—*whose; *viśadatām—*cheerfulness; *yāti—*attain; *manaḥ—*mind; *māna—*because of loving anger; *pariplavam—*restless.

**Which girl’s mind, restless due to loving anger, wouldn’t become cheerful after breaking all the rules upon hearing the melodious sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute?**

### Text 58

***navaṁ śīkaram āsādya***  
***yamānujāni-bhaṅgataḥ***  
***kasyāviśadatāṁ yāti***  
***mano māna-pariplavam***

*navam—*fresh; *śīkaram—*a drop of water; *āsādya—*after obtaining; *yamānujāni—*of the Yamunā (“whose birth occurred after Yama’s”); *bhaṅgataḥ—*on account of the waves; *kasya—*whose; *aviśadatām—*moroseness; *yāti—*attains; *manaḥ—*mind; *māna—*because of false pride, (or because of a fault); *pariplavam—*disturbed.

**After feeling a drop of water thrust from the Yamunā’s waves, whose mind, disturbed by false pride, could remain morose?**

*śloka-vṛttiḥ. ayam eva samudgakaḥ.*

The previous example was an instance of a repetition of a verse. This kind of *yamaka* is technically called a *samudgaka* (“a verse that is repeated with a different sense”).

### Text 59

*evaṁ viṁśatiḥ—*

Now twenty types of *yamakas* have been illustrated.

***ādy-anta-madhya-bhedena***  
***kramād atha samuccayāt***  
***antādi-bhedena punar***  
***bahudhā yamaka-kriyā***

*ādi—*beginning; *anta—*end; *madhya—*middle; *bhedena—*according to varieties; *kramāt—*according to the order; *atha—*now; *samuccayāt—*according to the combination; *anta—*end; *ādi—*beginning; *bhedena—*according to the variety; *punar—*furthermore; *bahu-dhā—*is of many kinds; *yamaka—*of a *yamaka; kriyā—*the making.

***Yamakas*** **are again of more kinds taking into account the following varieties: i)** ***ādi-yamaka*** **(a** ***yamaka*** **at the beginning of every line), ii)** ***anta-yamaka*** **(a** ***yamaka*** **at the end of every line), iii)** ***madhya-yamaka*** **(a** ***yamaka*** **in the middle of every line), iv)** ***ādy-anta-madhya-yamaka*** **(a** ***yamaka*** **at the beginning, middle, and end of every line), and v)** ***antādi-yamaka*** **(a** ***yamaka*** **at the beginning and end of every line).**

### Text 60

*krameṇodāharaṇāni, ādi-yamakaṁ yathā—*

The following examples are presented according to the order mentioned above. First an example of an *ādi-yamaka:*

***kalākalāpena garīyasā harir na dīna-dīptiḥ karuṇā-rasāmbudhiḥ***  
***surāsurāṇāṁ mukuṭāṭavī-maṇiḥ sadā sad-ānanda-cid-ātmako babhau***

*kalā—*of expertise in the arts; *kalāpena—*abundance; *garīyasā—*on account of a great; *hariḥ—*Hari; *na—*not; *dīna—*is of a low quality; *dīptiḥ—*one whose beauty; *karuṇā-rasa—*of compassion; *ambudhiḥ—*is an ocean; *sura-asurāṇām—*of the demigods and the demons; *mukuṭa—*crowns; *aṭavī—*among the many; *maṇiḥ—*is the jewel; *sadā—*always; *sat—*eternity; *ānanda—*bliss; *cid—*and consciousness; *ātmakaḥ—*whose body; *babhau—*appeared beautiful.

**Because of His great expertise in the arts, Hari is not lacking in beauty. \[Rather He is the most beautiful\]. He is an ocean of compassion and is the jewel atop the crowns the demons and the demigods. Being full of eternity, knowledge, and bliss, He always appears very beautiful.**

### Text 61

*anta-yamakaṁ yathā—*

Now an example of an *anta-yamaka:*

***roṣeṇa śaśvan na hi nāgarī garīyasā kaṭhoratvam upetya bhāty abhā***  
***vihāya mānaṁ harim ānayānayā dhiyā hi sarvaṁ bhavatīhitaṁ hitam***

*roṣeṇa—*anger; *śaśvat—*continuous; *na—*no; *hi—*indeed; *nāgarī—*female lover \[in *parakīya-rasa*\]; *garīyasā—*on account of great; *kaṭhoratvam—*hard-heartedness; *upetya—*after attaining; *bhāti—*shines; *abhā—*whose luster has disappeared; *vihāya—*abandoning; *mānam—*loving anger; *harim—*Hari; *ānaya—*bring; *anayā—*by means of this; *dhiyā—*wisdom; *hi—*because; *sarvam—*everything; *bhavati—*will soon be; *īhitam—*that is desired; *hitam—*and beneficial.

**No ladylove has ever appeared graceful by becoming continuously angry and hard-hearted. Therefore cease your huffiness and bring Hari close to you. In this way all your desires will come true.**

### Text 62

*madhya-yamakaṁ yathā—*

Now an example of a *madhya-yamaka:*

***madana-saṅgara-saṅga-rasākulā bhava vihāriṇi hāriṇi mādhave***  
***kusuma-rāji-virāji-vibhūṣaṇā madhupa-rāga-parāga-parācitā***

*madana—*of Cupid; *saṅgara—*during the battle; *saṅga—*of union; *rasa—*with the bliss*; ākulā—*filled; *bhava—*become; *vihāriṇi—*O playful girl; *hāriṇi—*is stealing \[your mind\]; *mādhave—*while Mādhava; *kusuma—*flowers; *rāji—*with many; *virāji—*are splendid; *vibhūṣaṇā—*whose ornaments; *madhupa—*of the bees; *rāga—*which causes the pleasure; *parāga—*with flower pollen; *parācitā—*pervaded.

**Your ornaments appear splendid with flower decorations, and You are personally smeared with flower pollen which causes the bees to become delighted.. O playful girl, become absorbed in the bliss of union while Mādhava is stealing your heart.**

### Text 63

*ādy-anta-madhya-yamakaṁ yathā—*

Now an example of a *ādy-anta-madhya-yamaka:*
